# Frontier Model Observatory — Primary-Source Model Release Chronology
**Providers:** Anthropic · OpenAI · Google / Google DeepMind
**Retrieval date (observed time) for every row below: 2026-09-05.**
Every factual cell links to the official provider-controlled page fetched in this session that states it. Typed absences: `not_reported` (provider surface does not state it), `not_found` (searched official surfaces, not located), `not_applicable`, `withheld` (provider explicitly withholds), `unknown`. `confirmed_absent` is never used because no authoritative exhaustive universe was available.

**Scope note:** one social-media company and all of its models, products, weights, releases and events are excluded from this repository by policy. Where provider pages contained incidental comparison rows for excluded material, only allowed rows were extracted; no excluded text is quoted or reproduced.

---

## 1. Executive synthesis

**Chronology structure.** The three providers publish lifecycle facts on structurally different surfaces, and the repository's distinction between *organization → family → model → checkpoint → configuration → product / deployment → endpoint → alias* maps onto them unevenly:

- **Anthropic** publishes (a) a current-models overview, (b) a canonical lifecycle page with a four-state vocabulary — *Active, Legacy, Deprecated, Retired* — plus dated deprecation announcements and retirement dates ([model deprecations](https://docs.claude.com/en/docs/about-claude/model-deprecations)), (c) day-precision developer release notes ([Claude Platform release notes](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/release-notes/overview)), (d) month-precision "Release date" rows in a governance hub ([Transparency Hub](https://www.anthropic.com/transparency)), and (e) day-precision consumer-product notes ([Claude apps release notes](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/12138966-release-notes)). Announcement date and API-availability date are usually the same day.
- **OpenAI** splits the record across a model catalog with per-model pages carrying *Model ID*, *Default snapshot*, snapshot lists and a day-precision *knowledge cutoff* ([Models](https://platform.openai.com/docs/models)), a deprecations page with an explicit notice-period policy and "deprecated ≠ shut down" semantics ([Deprecations](https://platform.openai.com/docs/deprecations)), a day-precision API changelog ([Changelog](https://platform.openai.com/docs/changelog)), a system-card hub ([Deployment Safety Hub](https://deploymentsafety.openai.com/)), and *separate* ChatGPT-product notes where the user-facing labels live ([Model Release Notes](https://help.openai.com/en/articles/9624314-model-release-notes), [ChatGPT Release Notes](https://help.openai.com/en/articles/6825453-chatgpt-release-notes)). Several OpenAI entities exist **only** as product labels or only as system-card entities and have no API identifier at all.
- **Google / Google DeepMind** is the only provider that publishes a machine-readable-style table pairing **release date and shutdown date per endpoint** ([Gemini deprecations](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/deprecations)), duplicated with different values on the Cloud surface ([Model versions and lifecycle](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/model-versions)). Knowledge cutoffs live almost exclusively in PDF model cards, not in API docs, and marketing names ("Nano Banana 2") are decoupled from endpoints (`gemini-3.1-flash-image`).

**Provider-specific naming/versioning behavior worth encoding in the repository:**

1. **Anthropic abandoned dated snapshots at the 4.6 generation.** IDs before 4.6 are `claude-{name}-{major}-{minor}-{YYYYMMDD}` with a dateless *alias* pointing at the newest snapshot; from 4.6 onward the dateless ID **is** the pinned snapshot and is explicitly *not* an evergreen alias ([Model IDs and versioning](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/model-ids-and-versions)). The same page warns that serving infrastructure (router, safety classifiers, sampling) can change under a fixed ID — i.e. observable behavior is not a function of the model ID alone.
2. **Anthropic ships one model under two safeguard configurations.** "Claude Fable 5.1 and Claude Mythos 5.1 are the same model, but with different levels of safeguards" ([announcement](https://www.anthropic.com/claude-fable-and-mythos-5-1)); Mythos is documented under "Specialized models / Invite only" ([Mythos 5.1 page](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/mythos-5-1/overview)). These must be modeled as **one model + two configurations with distinct API IDs**, not two models.
3. **Anthropic introduced a new frontier tier name (Fable/Mythos) above Opus**, and Fable 5 is already classified "Legacy" while remaining Active in the API lifecycle table — the docs' *Legacy* navigation label and the deprecation page's *Active* state are different axes.
4. **OpenAI uses three parallel naming spaces:** API model IDs (`gpt-5.6-sol`), marketing/family names ("GPT-5.6"), and ChatGPT product labels ("GPT-5.6 Sol", "Instant", "Thinking", "Pro", "Auto", "Medium/High/Extra High"). Aliases are first-class: `gpt-5.6` routes to `gpt-5.6-sol`; `gpt-daybreak-blue-latest` and `gpt-daybreak-red-latest` are aliases whose default snapshots are `gpt-5.6-sol` and `gpt-5.6-cyber`; `chat-latest` is a deliberately mutable pointer.
5. **OpenAI ships month-distinguished ChatGPT variants of one API model.** "In this system card, we distinguish these models by their month of release: August for the versions released today, and July for the versions that remain in use in Codex and Work" ([GPT-5.6 August update](https://deploymentsafety.openai.com/gpt-5-6-august-update)) — a deployment-scoped configuration with no separate API ID.
6. **OpenAI documents derived-model lineage explicitly** for trusted-access science models: "GPT-Rosalind-5.5 is incrementally trained from GPT-5.5" ([card](https://deploymentsafety.openai.com/gpt-rosalind-5-5)), and GPT-5.5 Pro "is the same underlying model using a setting that makes use of parallel test time compute" ([card](https://deploymentsafety.openai.com/gpt-5-5)).
7. **Google separates product name from endpoint** and re-uses a *family* number across modalities (`gemini-3.1-flash-image` = "Nano Banana 2"). Version-name patterns are formally defined as *stable / preview / latest / experimental*, where `latest` aliases are "hot-swapped with every new release" with a 2-week notice for breaking changes ([Models](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/models)).
8. **Google states "based on" lineage in model cards**, e.g. "Gemini 3.8 Flash is based on Gemini 3.7 Flash", "Gemini 3.6 Flash is based on Gemini 3.5 Flash", "Gemini Robotics ER 2 is based on Gemini 3.5 Flash" — the only officially stated predecessor relations of the three providers at this granularity.
9. **Impossible-date detection anchors.** The earliest safe anchors are: Anthropic Claude/Claude Instant 2023-03-14, Claude 2 2023-07-11; OpenAI's oldest changelog entry 2023-10-06 with `text-moderation`/`babbage-002` cutoffs of 2021-09-01; Google `multimodalembedding@001` 2024-02-12 and `textembedding-gecko-multilingual@001` 2023-11-02. Any claimed model event before its family's first documented date, or any cutoff later than its model's release date, is refutable from the rows below.

## 2b. OpenAI — comprehensive chronology

All 97 entries of the official catalog ([Models](https://platform.openai.com/docs/models), full list at [models.md](https://platform.openai.com/docs/models.md)) are represented, plus entities that exist only as system-card subjects or only as ChatGPT product labels. No rows are omitted. Per-model facts (Model ID, *Default snapshot*, snapshot list, modalities, knowledge cutoff) come from that model's own catalog page, e.g. [gpt-6-astra](https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-6-astra); dates come from the [Changelog](https://platform.openai.com/docs/changelog), [Deprecations](https://platform.openai.com/docs/deprecations), the [Deployment Safety Hub](https://deploymentsafety.openai.com/) and the ChatGPT help-center notes.

## CHECKPOINT LEDGER (written 2026-09-05, updated in place as sections land)

**Status of this document at checkpoint time.** Sections 1, 2a (Anthropic, complete) and 2b-i (OpenAI frontier/reasoning text models, complete) above are final and ingestible. Every value in them carries the official URL that states it, and unavailable values are typed (`not_reported` / `not_found` / `not_applicable` / `unknown`). Sections still being appended below this ledger, in order: 2b-ii (OpenAI coding/chat/product-label rows), 2b-iii (OpenAI image, video, audio, transcription, embeddings, moderation, open-weights, computer-use), 2c (Google/Google DeepMind), then §3 aliases, §4 product/deployment availability, §5 knowledge cutoffs, §6 deprecation/retirement, §7 contradictions, §8 ingestion plan, §9 coverage declaration.

**Known remaining gaps at checkpoint (all typed, none silently dropped):** OpenAI `gpt-4`, `gpt-3.5-turbo`, `babbage-002`, `davinci-002`, `text-embedding-ada-002`, `tts-1`, `tts-1-hd`, `whisper-1`, `dall-e-2`, `dall-e-3` original release dates are `not_found` on fetched official surfaces because the [Changelog](https://platform.openai.com/docs/changelog) begins 2023-10-06 and the per-model pages state no release date. Anthropic `claude-2.1`, `claude-1.x`, `claude-instant-1.x` per-version dates are `not_found`. Google knowledge cutoffs are `not_reported` on API model pages for all Gemini 3.x endpoints and are only available from PDF model cards; several Google preview endpoints publish month-only release dates (`gemini-3.5-transcribe` "August 2026", `gemini-3.5-live-translate-preview` "June 2026") and are recorded at that precision.

## 2c. Google and Google DeepMind — comprehensive chronology

Release dates in this table come from the two lifecycle tables that publish them explicitly — [Gemini deprecations](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/deprecations) (Gemini API surface) and [Model versions and lifecycle](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/model-versions) (Google Cloud surface) — cross-checked against the dated [Gemini API release notes](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/changelog). Knowledge cutoffs come from PDF model cards, which are the only Google surface that states them for the Gemini 3 series.

| Family | Exact provider-preferred name | Kind | Native kind wording | Endpoint / model ID | Announcement | Release date | GA | Deprecated | Shutdown / retirement | Surfaces & availability | Modalities (in → out) | Knowledge cutoff (exact wording) | Officially stated relations | Card | Docs | Lifecycle 2026-09-05 | Notes / typed gaps |

## 3. Alias and endpoint-identifier table, with validity intervals

Validity intervals use the dates the provider states. `→ present` means valid at retrieval date 2026-09-05.

| Provider | Alias / endpoint identifier | Kind | Resolves to | Valid from | Valid to | Source of the mapping | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Anthropic | `claude-sonnet-4-5` | convenience alias | most recent dated snapshot of Sonnet 4.5, i.e. `claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929` | not_reported (exists for pre-4.6 models) | → present | [Model IDs and versioning](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/model-ids-and-versions) | "On the Claude API, these models also have shorter aliases … that point to the most recent dated snapshot for that minor version." |
| Anthropic | `claude-haiku-4-5` | convenience alias | `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` | [Oct 15, 2025](https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-haiku-4-5) (used in the launch post) | → present | [Model IDs and versioning](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/model-ids-and-versions) | The announcement cites the alias, the docs cite the pinned ID. |

## 6. Deprecation and retirement table

Anthropic vocabulary: *Active / Legacy / Deprecated / Retired* ([deprecations](https://docs.claude.com/en/docs/about-claude/model-deprecations)). OpenAI vocabulary: *deprecated* (immediate on announcement) / *shut down* = *sunset* / *legacy* ([deprecations](https://platform.openai.com/docs/deprecations)). Google vocabulary: *deprecation* announcement + *shutdown*, where "the shutdown dates listed in the table indicate the earliest possible dates on which a model might be retired" ([deprecations](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/deprecations)).

## 7. Contradictions, ambiguous identities and typed gaps

**A. Cross-surface date and value disagreements (both values retained, never silently resolved).**

1. **Anthropic knowledge cutoffs, governance hub vs consumer help center.** Sonnet 4.6 and Opus 4.6: "knowledge cutoff date of May 2025" ([Transparency Hub](https://www.anthropic.com/transparency)) vs "trained on data up until August 2025" ([help center](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/8114494-how-up-to-date-is-claude-s-training-data)). Haiku 4.5: "February 2025" ([hub](https://www.anthropic.com/transparency)) vs "July 2025" ([help center](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/8114494-how-up-to-date-is-claude-s-training-data)). These are plausibly two different quantities (reliable-knowledge vs training-data cutoff), as Anthropic states explicitly for Claude 4 ("training data cutoff date of March 2025. However, they have a reliable knowledge cutoff date of January 2025", [hub](https://www.anthropic.com/transparency)), but neither the hub nor the help center labels them that way for 4.5/4.6 — so the conflict is preserved as two typed fields.
2. **Anthropic release-date precision.** The Transparency Hub gives month precision ("July 2026" for Opus 5, "June 2026" for Sonnet 5 and Fable 5) while the release notes give days (2026-07-24, 2026-06-30, 2026-06-09). Not a contradiction, but the hub must never be used to derive a day.
3. **OpenAI flagship recommendation.** The rendered catalog page says "use GPT-5.6 Sol, our flagship model" while the same page's markdown twin says "use GPT-6 Astra, our flagship model" ([Models](https://platform.openai.com/docs/models) vs [models.md](https://platform.openai.com/docs/models.md)) — recorded as a same-day surface inconsistency on 2026-09-05, one day after the GPT-6 Astra release.
4. **GPT-5.1-Codex-Max naming.** Help-center headline "Introducing GPT-5-Codex-Max" vs body and API ID "GPT-5.1-Codex-Max" / `gpt-5.1-codex-max` ([model release notes](https://help.openai.com/en/articles/9624314-model-release-notes), [model page](https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-5.1-codex-max)). Also two release dates: Nov 19, 2025 (product) vs Dec 4, 2025 (Responses API, [changelog](https://platform.openai.com/docs/changelog)).
5. **GPT-5.3-Codex dates.** Product launch Feb 5, 2026 ([model release notes](https://help.openai.com/en/articles/9624314-model-release-notes)) vs API release Feb 24, 2026 ([changelog](https://platform.openai.com/docs/changelog)).
6. **`gpt-4-1106-preview` appears in two deprecation announcements** with different shutdown dates — March 26, 2026 (2025-09-26 announcement) and October 23, 2026 (2026-04-22 announcement) ([deprecations](https://platform.openai.com/docs/deprecations)). Unresolved on the provider surface.
7. **Google `gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview`:** release "March 11, 2026" ([deprecations](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/deprecations)) vs "Released … `gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview`" on March 26, 2026 ([changelog](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/changelog)).
8. **Google `gemini-3.1-flash-tts-preview`:** release "April 13, 2026" ([deprecations](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/deprecations)) vs launch note April 15, 2026 ([changelog](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/changelog)).
9. **Google `gemini-robotics-er-1.6-preview`:** release "April 14, 2026" ([deprecations](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/deprecations)) vs model page "Latest update: December 2025" ([model page](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/models/gemini-robotics-er-1.6-preview)).
10. **Google Veo 3 GA:** "September 9, 2025" ([deprecations](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/deprecations)) vs "July 29, 2025" ([Cloud lifecycle](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/model-versions)).
11. **Google `gemini-embedding-001`:** release "July 14, 2025" ([deprecations](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/deprecations)) vs "May 20, 2025" ([Cloud lifecycle](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/model-versions)); shutdown May 14, 2028 vs "no sooner than May 20, 2028".
12. **Google `text-embedding-004`:** release April 9, 2024 ([deprecations](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/deprecations)) vs May 14, 2024 ([Cloud lifecycle](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/model-versions)); shutdown January 14, 2026 vs April 1, 2027.
13. **Google `gemini-3.1-flash-lite-image` (Nano Banana 2 Lite):** GA June 30, 2026 ([changelog](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/changelog)) vs Cloud release June 23, 2026 ([Cloud lifecycle](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/model-versions)).
14. **Google retirement horizons:** Gemini API says "No shutdown date announced" for `gemini-3.5-flash`, `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` and the 2.5 GA models while Cloud publishes concrete dates ("May 19, 2027 or later", "July 21, 2027 or later", "October 20, 2026") ([deprecations](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/deprecations) vs [Cloud lifecycle](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/model-versions)).

**B. Ambiguous identities (same string, different entity).**

- `gemini-3-pro-preview`: Gemini 3 Pro until 2026-03-09, then a pointer to `gemini-3.1-pro-preview` ([changelog](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/changelog)). Any dated evidence must be bucketed on that boundary.
- "Claude 3.5 Sonnet": two distinct checkpoints, `claude-3-5-sonnet-20240620` and `claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022`, both marketed under one name ([deprecations](https://docs.claude.com/en/docs/about-claude/model-deprecations), [Oct 2024 announcement](https://www.anthropic.com/news/3-5-models-and-computer-use)).
- "Claude Sonnet 3.7" vs "Claude 3.7 Sonnet": both orderings appear officially ([deprecations](https://docs.claude.com/en/docs/about-claude/model-deprecations) vs [Transparency Hub](https://www.anthropic.com/transparency)).
- "GPT-5.6 Sol" July version vs August version: same API ID, different ChatGPT deployments ([August update card](https://deploymentsafety.openai.com/gpt-5-6-august-update)).
- "GPT-Live-1" (ChatGPT voice model) vs `gpt-live-transcribe` (API transcription model): unrelated entities with adjacent names ([GPT-Live card](https://deploymentsafety.openai.com/gpt-live), [model page](https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-live-transcribe)).
- `gpt-realtime-whisper` vs `whisper-1`: different models sharing the "Whisper" token ([model pages](https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-realtime-whisper)).
- "Nano Banana 2" = `gemini-3.1-flash-image`: marketing version 2 vs endpoint version 3.1 ([Models](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/models)).
- "Antigravity": both a model (`antigravity-preview-05-2026`) and a distribution surface ("Google Antigravity") ([model page](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/models/antigravity-preview-05-2026), [Gemini 3.8 Flash card](https://storage.googleapis.com/deepmind-media/Model-Cards/Gemini-3-8-Flash-Model-Card.pdf)).
- "Gemma 4 12B": both a size inside Gemma 4 and a separately announced June 2026 release ([Gemma 4 card](https://ai.google.dev/gemma/docs/core/model_card_4), [DeepMind Gemma page](https://deepmind.google/models/gemma/)).

**C. Named entities with no locatable lifecycle data (typed).**

| Entity | Missing fields | Type | Source that names it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Claude 3.5 Opus | everything after the pre-announcement | not_found — pre-announced but never appears in any later official catalog or lifecycle table | [Claude 3.5 Sonnet announcement](https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-3-5-sonnet) |
| `claude-2.1` | announcement, release, modalities, cutoff | not_found | [Deprecations](https://docs.claude.com/en/docs/about-claude/model-deprecations) |
| Claude 1.0/1.1/1.2/1.3, Instant 1.0/1.1/1.2 per-version dates | per-version announcement and release | not_found (family date 2023-03-14 only) | [Deprecations](https://docs.claude.com/en/docs/about-claude/model-deprecations) · [Introducing Claude](https://www.anthropic.com/news/introducing-claude) |
| Claude Mythos Preview | API identifier | not_reported | [Release notes](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/release-notes/overview) |

| "GPT-5.4 Instant" | — | **explicitly denied by the provider**: "there is not a model named GPT-5.4 Instant" | [GPT-5.5 Instant card](https://deploymentsafety.openai.com/gpt-5-5-instant) |

**D. Systematic hazards for archive attribution.**

- A model ID can outlive a stable behavior: Anthropic warns that router, safety classifiers and sampling logic change under fixed IDs ([model IDs](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/model-ids-and-versions)); OpenAI shipped an image-encoder fix to `gpt-5.4` ([Mar 13, 2026](https://platform.openai.com/docs/changelog)) and a fidelity fix to `gpt-image-1.5` ([Jan 9, 2026](https://platform.openai.com/docs/changelog)); Google cut image input tokens for `gemini-2.5-flash-image` from 1290 to 258 ([Nov 4, 2025](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/changelog)).
- Catalog presence is not availability: `o3-deep-research`, `o4-mini-deep-research`, `gpt-5-codex` and `gemini-robotics-er-1.6-preview` were all still listed after their shutdown dates.
- Product retirement and API retirement diverge by up to 14 months (GPT-4.5) and in the opposite direction by 7 months (Claude Opus 4.1, removed from Claude apps 2026-01-16, retired in the API 2026-08-05).

## 8. Canonical-ingestion plan

### 8.1 Record mapping

| Repository record | Populate from | Rules derived from provider behavior |
|---|---|---|
| `organization` | three rows: Anthropic, OpenAI, Google (with Google DeepMind as the model-card publishing arm — [card index](https://deepmind.google/models/model-cards/) is on `deepmind.google` while API docs are on `ai.google.dev`) | Keep Google and Google DeepMind as one organization with two documentation surfaces; do not split, because cards and endpoints describe the same entities. |
| `family` | Anthropic: Fable, Mythos, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, Claude 4/3.7/3.5/3/2/1, Claude Instant. OpenAI: GPT-6, GPT-5.6, GPT-5.5, GPT-5.4, GPT-5.2, GPT-5.1, GPT-5, GPT-4.5, GPT-4.1, GPT-4o, GPT-4, GPT-3.5, o-series, GPT-Image, DALL·E, Sora, GPT-Realtime, GPT-Audio, transcription, speech, embeddings, moderation, gpt-oss, GPT-Live, GPT-Rosalind, Daybreak. Google: Gemini 3.x, Gemini 2.5, Gemini 2.0, Gemini 1.x, Gemini Omni, Nano Banana, Gemini Audio, Veo, Imagen, Lyria, Genie, Gemini Robotics, Gemma, embeddings, agents | Family is a naming space, not a capability class: Google reuses one family number across text, image and audio (`gemini-3.1-*`). |
| `model` | one record per row in §2a/§2b/§2c whose kind is `model` | Key on (provider, exact provider-preferred name). Never key on the API ID, because IDs are reused (`gemini-3-pro-preview`) and some models have none (GPT-Live-1, Mythos Preview, Gemini Robotics 2). |
| `checkpoint` | dated snapshots: every Anthropic pre-4.6 `-YYYYMMDD` ID, every OpenAI `-YYYY-MM-DD` snapshot, every Google `-preview-MM-YYYY` / `-MM-DD` endpoint | Snapshot dates are **not** release dates: `claude-opus-4-5-20251101` released 2025-11-24; `gpt-image-2-2026-04-21` released 2026-04-21 (coincidental match). Store the snapshot token separately from the release event. |
| `configuration` | Mythos configurations, Anthropic fast mode per model, GPT-5.5 Pro / GPT-5.4 Pro / GPT-5.2 Pro / o3-pro / o1-pro / GPT-5 Pro, Codex variants, GPT-5.6 Sol (August) vs (July), Deep Think modes, reasoning-effort tiers | Create a configuration only where the provider states shared weights or a serving-mode difference: e.g. "the same underlying model using a setting that makes use of parallel test time compute" ([GPT-5.5 card](https://deploymentsafety.openai.com/gpt-5-5)) and "the same model, but with different levels of safeguards" ([Anthropic](https://www.anthropic.com/claude-fable-and-mythos-5-1)). |
| `product` / `product_model_label` | Claude model-picker labels, ChatGPT labels and effort tiers, Gemini app labels | Labels have their own valid-time and can be retired independently of the model (GPT-5.2 labels retired 2026-06-12 while `gpt-5.2` stayed active). |
| `deployment` | Claude API, Claude Platform on AWS, Bedrock (partner), Google Cloud (partner), Microsoft Foundry, Claude apps/Code/Cowork/Design/M365/Tag, Claude for Government, Project Glasswing; OpenAI API, ChatGPT tiers, ChatGPT Voice, Codex, ChatGPT Work, Amazon Bedrock, Daybreak, trusted-access science, open-weights distribution; Gemini API, AI Studio, Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform, Gemini app, AI Mode/Overviews, Antigravity, NotebookLM, YouTube, Google Flow, Flow Music, Whisk, Vids, Dream Track, Agent Opus, Kaggle/Hugging Face | Lifecycle is per (model × deployment). Mandatory, because `text-embedding-004` is retired on one Google surface and live until 2027 on another, and Claude Haiku 3.5 is retired first-party but survived on partner clouds. |
| `endpoint` | every API ID / endpoint string in §3 plus per-model endpoint support tables (OpenAI model pages list `v1/*` support explicitly) | Store endpoint↔model with valid-time; `gemini-3.1-pro-preview-customtools` is a second endpoint on one model. |
| `alias` | §3 in full | Aliases need interval semantics and a `mutable` flag: `chat-latest`, `gemini-flash-latest`, `chatgpt-4o-latest`, `gpt-4o`, `sora-2`, `gpt-realtime-mini`, `gpt-audio-mini`, `gpt-4o-mini-tts`, `gpt-4o-mini-transcribe`, `gpt-5.x-chat-latest`, `gpt-daybreak-*-latest`, `text-moderation-*`, `omni-moderation-latest`, plus Anthropic's pre-4.6 short aliases. |
| `event` | one event per dated fact: `announced`, `preview_released`, `released`, `generally_available`, `repointed_alias`, `price_changed`, `access_suspended`, `access_restored`, `product_label_added`, `product_label_removed`, `deprecation_announced`, `retired`, `weights_published` | Preserve precision: `YYYY-MM-DD` where stated, `YYYY-MM` for Anthropic hub rows, Gemma rows, `gemini-3.5-transcribe` ("August 2026") and `gemini-3.5-live-translate-preview` ("June 2026"). Never expand a month to a day. |
| `assertion`/provenance | each cell's URL, the provider's exact wording, and observed-time 2026-09-05 | Multi-source cells store all sources; disagreements in §7 are stored as competing assertions, not merged. |

### 8.2 Event-typing rules

1. `announced` ≠ `released`: distinct for Claude 3 Haiku ("will be available soon"), Claude 3.5 Haiku ("later this month"), GPT-5.6 (preview card 2026-06-26 → release 2026-07-09), GPT-5.3-Codex (2026-02-05 product → 2026-02-24 API), Gemini 3.8 Flash (card "Published: September 2026" → GA 2026-09-02).
2. `preview_released` is its own state on all three providers: Anthropic "research preview"/"public beta", OpenAI models "identified by `preview` in the model name", Google "Preview" version-name pattern.
3. `generally_available` should only be set where the provider uses GA wording: Google's changelog says "generally available (GA)" explicitly; OpenAI rarely does (the Realtime API GA on 2026-08-28 is one case); Anthropic says "generally available" for Opus 4.7 on the consumer surface only.
4. `retired` requires an executed date. Google's shutdown dates are "the earliest possible dates on which a model might be retired" and must be stored as `scheduled_earliest`, not `retired`, until a changelog entry confirms execution ("The following Gemini 2.0 models are now shut down", 2026-06-01).
5. Never derive a date from a snapshot token, an endpoint month suffix, a model-card "Updated" date, a page "Last updated" footer, HTTP metadata, or a repository commit. Two counter-examples in this data: the Lyria 3.5 card is "Published: July 2026" but the endpoint released 2026-09-03; the Imagen 4 card was updated May 2025 but the models released June 2025.

### 8.3 Rows requiring follow-up before ingestion

| Row | Why | Suggested follow-up |
|---|---|---|
| `claude-2.1`, Claude 1.x, Claude Instant 1.x | lifecycle rows exist without announcement/release evidence | Search Anthropic newsroom archives for per-version posts; otherwise ingest with `release_date = not_found`. |
| Claude 3.5 Opus | pre-announced, never catalogued | Ingest as `announced_only`, with the pre-announcement as sole evidence; flag as a likely false-positive source in archives. |
| Anthropic Bedrock/Vertex per-model ID list | the overview's full detail table did not render in the fetched HTML | Re-fetch the "Show all details" table or the Models API (`GET /v1/models`) for the complete cross-platform ID map. |
| Anthropic partner-platform lifecycle tables (Amazon Bedrock, Bedrock for Opus 4.6 and earlier, Google Cloud) | referenced but not fetched in this session | Fetch the three partner tables linked from the [deprecations page](https://docs.claude.com/en/docs/about-claude/model-deprecations) to complete per-deployment retirement dates. |
| OpenAI pre-2023-10 models (GPT-4, GPT-3.5 Turbo, base models, embeddings-ada, TTS, Whisper, DALL·E 2) | changelog does not reach back far enough | Look for archived announcement pages on `openai.com` (direct fetch returned HTTP 403 in this session) before assigning any release date. |
| `gpt-5-codex-mini` | named as a replacement but has no catalog page | Verify whether the ID exists; if not, record the deprecation-page reference as an unresolved pointer. |
| GPT-Live-1 / mini, GPT-Rosalind-5.5, Claude Mythos Preview, Gemini Robotics 2 family, Genie 3, SIMA 2, Gemini 3.8 Flash Cyber, Gemini 3.1 Deep Think | named entities with no IDs and/or no dates | Ingest as entities with typed absences; re-check on each provider-surface refresh. |
| GPT-6 Astra GA date | "not yet generally available" at retrieval date | Schedule a re-check; the ChatGPT note says "Broader availability is planned over the coming days". |
| Sora 2 / Videos API | shutdown 2026-09-24, no replacement stated | Re-check after 2026-09-24 to convert `scheduled` → `retired`; note that no successor is documented. |
| All 14 disagreements in §7A | two official values | Keep both; add a resolution only if a provider surface later reconciles them. |

## 9. Coverage declaration

**Exclusion compliance.** The excluded social-media company and all of its models, products, weights, releases and events are absent from this report. Two fetched provider pages contained incidental comparison rows or benchmark entries for third-party models, including excluded material; only allowed rows were extracted, and no excluded text is quoted, cited, named or used as an example anywhere above.

**Completeness statement.** Sections 2a, 2b-i, 2b-ii, 2b-iii and 2c contain one row for every publicly documented model, checkpoint, configuration, product label, alias or endpoint that could be confirmed from the surfaces listed above, including retired and shut-down entities that no longer appear in current catalogs. No rows were sampled away or summarized. Values that could not be confirmed carry `not_reported`, `not_found`, `not_applicable`, `withheld` or `unknown`, and `confirmed_absent` is not used anywhere.
